Or I'll walk all the way to the supply closet and completely forget what I even went in there for. I just can't seem to focus my thoughts lately.\n\nDoctor: {\"intent\":\"Determine when the concentration problems began to distinguish lifelong ADHD from a recent mood, sleep, anxiety, or cognitive change.\",\"question\":\"When did you first notice these problems with concentration and memory?\"]\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the contractor I work for changed our schedules. They put us on these rotating evening and overnight shifts at the larger properties, and that's when I really started noticing the issues with my focus.\n\nDoctor: Do your concentration problems become worse during or after your overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's definitely worse after the overnights. My sleep has been messed up for a long time now. Like, after an overnight shift, I’ll just lie there in bed... I'm so tired, but my brain is just wired. And even on the days when I do manage to sleep a decent number of hours, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. So yeah, the focus is definitely worse when I'm dealing with that.\n\nDoctor: Do your sleep and concentration improve when you have several days without overnight or rotating shifts?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not really sure. I don't usually get several days off in a row from that schedule to really find out. But even when I do get a regular weekend or a couple of normal days, I haven't noticed much of an improvement. I still wake up feeling just as tired, and my head is still pretty foggy.\n\nDoctor: Before your work schedule changed, did you have similar problems with concentration or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: No, I really didn't. Before they switched my schedule, I didn't have any trouble getting my work done or remembering what I needed to do. It all just seemed to start when the shifts changed.\n\nDoctor: When you are able to keep a regular daytime schedule for several days, do you still have difficulty falling asleep at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, my sleep has just been messed up for a long time now. Even if I get a few normal daytime shifts in a row, it doesn't really fix it. I might not be quite as tired but wired as I am right after an overnight shift, but I still struggle to get to sleep. And even when I do manage to drift off and get a decent number of hours at night, I still wake up the next morning feeling like I barely rested at all. It just never really feels like I catch up.\n\nDoctor: When you are free to sleep on your preferred schedule, without needing to wake for work, does your sleep become restful?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, not really. It doesn't seem to matter if I don't have an alarm set or anywhere to be. My sleep has just been messed up for a long time now. Even on days where I can sleep on my own schedule and I actually manage to get a decent number of hours in... I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. It's like my body is just stuck in that tired but wired mode from the overnights, and sleeping in doesn't really fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you try to sleep at your intended bedtime, do you feel sleepy but remain unable to fall asleep, or do you mainly not feel sleepy until much later?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I'm pretty much always exhausted, so I feel like I should be sleepy. But when I actually get in bed... I don't know, I haven't really paid attention to whether I actually feel sleepy right at that moment or if it hits me later. I just know I lay there and can't seem to drift off.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not have to wake up for work, what time do you naturally start feeling sleepy enough to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don't really have a set time, honestly. Since my schedule is so all over the place with the night shifts, I don't think my body even knows when it's supposed to be tired anymore. Even on my days off, I just feel that same constant exhaustion, but I couldn't tell you a specific hour when I naturally feel ready to sleep. It's just a blur.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It all just seemed to start when the shifts changed.\n\nDoctor: When you are able to keep a regular daytime schedule for several days, do you still have difficulty falling asleep at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, my sleep has just been messed up for a long time now. Even if I get a few normal daytime shifts in a row, it doesn't really fix it. I might not be quite as tired but wired as I am right after an overnight shift, but I still struggle to get to sleep. And even when I do manage to drift off and get a decent number of hours at night, I still wake up the next morning feeling like I barely rested at all. It just never really feels like I catch up.\n\nDoctor: When you are free to sleep on your preferred schedule, without needing to wake for work, does your sleep become restful?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, not really. It doesn't seem to matter if I don't have an alarm set or anywhere to be. My sleep has just been messed up for a long time now. Even on days where I can sleep on my own schedule and I actually manage to get a decent number of hours in... I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. It's like my body is just stuck in that tired but wired mode from the overnights, and sleeping in doesn't really fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you try to sleep at your intended bedtime, do you feel sleepy but remain unable to fall asleep, or do you mainly not feel sleepy until much later?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I'm pretty much always exhausted, so I feel like I should be sleepy. But when I actually get in bed... I don't know, I haven't really paid attention to whether I actually feel sleepy right at that moment or if it hits me later. I just know I lay there and can't seem to drift off.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not have to wake up for work, what time do you naturally start feeling sleepy enough to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don't really have a set time, honestly. Since my schedule is so all over the place with the night shifts, I don't think my body even knows when it's supposed to be tired anymore. Even on my days off, I just feel that same constant exhaustion, but I couldn't tell you a specific hour when I naturally feel ready to sleep. It's just a blur.\n\nDoctor: When you have several days without work or an alarm, do you still lie awake for a long time before falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my sleep has just been messed up for a long time now. It doesn't really matter if I have a few days off in a row. After working those overnight shifts, I'll just lie there in bed... tired but wired, you know? And even if I eventually fall asleep and get a decent number of hours, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. Having days off doesn't seem to reset it.\n\nDoctor: In a typical week, on how many nights do you lie awake for a long time before falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don't know exactly how many nights. It feels like almost all of them, to be honest. I haven't really kept track of a specific number, but it's definitely most nights out of the week that I'm just lying there, waiting to fall asleep.\n\nDoctor: After a night when you feel you slept well and wake feeling rested, do you still have the same difficulty concentrating during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it doesn't really seem to make a difference how I sleep. Honestly, that's kind of why I came in... my head just doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory at work or trying to study for this supervisor certification I want, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way to the supply closet and completely forget what I went in there for. It just happens all the time now, no matter if I slept okay or not.\n\nDoctor: What is the longest period you have had only daytime shifts, and during that period did you still have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't think I've had a period of just daytime shifts. I've been on the overnight cleaning crew for years now. So... I'm not really sure. I haven't had a stretch of normal day hours to compare it to, so I couldn't tell you if I'd still have trouble falling asleep or not.\n\nDoctor: After an overnight shift, when you try to sleep during the daytime, do you usually have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Honestly, my sleep has been messed up for a really long time now. I'll get home from a shift and just lie there in bed... I'll be completely tired, but I just feel wired, you know? And the frustrating part is that even when I do manage to sleep a decent number of hours during the day, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My sleep has been messed up for a long time now. Like, after an overnight shift, I’ll just lie there in bed... I'm so tired, but my brain is just wired. Even on days where I can sleep on my own schedule and I actually manage to get a decent number of hours in... I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. It's like my body is just stuck in that tired but wired mode from the overnights, and sleeping in doesn't really fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you try to sleep at your intended bedtime, do you feel sleepy but remain unable to fall asleep, or do you mainly not feel sleepy until much later?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest.
